# Component migration order

Proposed 24 October 2026, for agreement. The bottom-up order in which the GWT client moves to dumb components and controllers (see `COMPONENT_MODEL_DESIGN.md`, "Components, controllers and models"): every widget first, then composites, then dialogs and lists, then tabs, document editors, screens and the shell.

> **Update, 24 October 2026:** `data_grid2` and `copy_and_paste` have been merged to master, and master into this branch, so nothing waits for them any more; the *In flight* column is history. "Second review, after merging master" below says what changed.

## Second review, after merging master (24 October 2026)

A second review by 24 agents checked the plan against the code after master (with `data_grid2` and `copy_and_paste`) was merged in, and planned every unit's names and styles. Six re-reviewed the 182 units the merge touched and the classes it added; 17 (one per wave, two for each of the largest) planned each unit's component and controller names (checked against every class name in the repository), where its template goes, what it deletes (view interface, `ViewImpl`, `UiHandlers`, GIN binding lines), every inline style with its replacement, everything that can move into its template, and what must stay in Java; a critic checked a sample of 50 plans against the code and the CSS. The per-unit plans are in `component-migration/wave-NN.md`, linked from each wave below.

**What the merge changed.** For most units, only renames (`DataGridWidget` is `DataGrid`; `stroom.data.grid.client` is `stroom.widget.datagrid.client`; `ClipboardUtil.copy` is `Clipboard.writeText`). Gone with GWT's cell tables: `HoverActionMenuCell`, `CustomCellList`, `FindCellTable` and the explorer's `SelectionEventManager`; `Refreshable` is now just an interface, not UI. New: the `Menu` widget (dumb, needing a tidy; wave 5, before `MenuPresenter`), `MenuManager` (converted with `MenuPresenter` in wave 6, making a `MenuController`), and, not UI, `ContextMenuManager` (app-wide infrastructure, like `MenuManager`), `TextEditMenu`, `TextEditTarget`, `TextField`, `CodeEditorText`, `Clipboard`, `GridEvents` and `RowWritePlan`. The grid, the code editor and the expression editors now add the field's edit items to their context menus: more context-menu logic to split between component and controller in waves 5, 6, 8 and 13. `WrapperView`, `AbstractPopupPanel` and the dialog chrome are back in wave 3 (they waited only for `data_grid2`); `PagerView` and `PagerViewWithHeading` stay in wave 5, merged into one `PagerPanel` with the grid.

**The work found.** 299 inline styles (226 fixed, to Stroom CSS classes; 73 runtime values, to custom properties); and, into templates, 569 component declarations, 253 pieces of fixed layout built in Java, 175 CSS classes, 170 ARIA attributes and 92 pieces of text.

**Corrections from the check** (the wave files are written from the plans; these override them):

- Names agree across waves: `PagerView` and `PagerViewWithHeading` become `PagerPanel` (declared `<dg:PagerPanel>`) and `HeadedPagerPanel` (wave 5 kept the heading's own panel, see `component-migration/wave-05.md`); a picker is a `DropDownBox` in the template with a controller attached (`DocSelectionController`, `UserRefSelectionController`, `DocumentAssetSelectionController`), never a `DocSelectionBox` or `UserRefSelectionBox` class; the notification lists merge into `NotificationList` and `NotificationListController`; `SecretPresenter` is renamed `SecretForm` with its five implementers; `QueryDataPane` is one shared component (`QueryDataViewImpl`'s four subclasses are empty), and the four data presenters become controllers of it; `MenuPresenter` becomes `MenuController`, beside `Menu`, the widget, which it shows (wave 6). Plans refer to other units by id, and ids were reused between waves: go by name.
- CSS classes that don't exist: `toolbar-grid` (new, in the floor map's shared toolbar-above-grid shell, keeping `DockLayoutPanel`'s 26px toolbar band), `dashboard-settings` (the settings panes share `settingsPresenter` in `components.css`), `flex-layout__item` (new, in `FlexLayout`'s unit, which sets the positions). `value-spinner--wide` must beat `.stroom-font-size-large .value-spinner` (`value-spinner.css:35`), or the large-text preference shrinks it. `DashboardViewImpl`'s inline `top: 0` is dead: drop it rather than add a class.
- Not fixed after all, so they stay in Java: the spinner's busy text (put into its live region as it shows), `CredentialsManager`'s label visibility, `XSDDisplayBox`'s optional content panel, `PagerViewWithHeading`'s task monitor (it spins the refresh button: kept, in `HeadedPagerPanel`, as in `PagerPanel`), the `<br/>` heading of expander columns (it sets the header's height), `default-min-sizes` on the expression dialog (per use), and `FloorMapIconPicker.setEnabled` (it means "not in use", not read only).
- Template limits: UiBinder has no `long` parser, so `ValueSpinner` gets `int` setters with their own names for templates (`setMin`/`setMax` take `long`); `ExpressionTreePanel` and `DraggableTreePanel` take constructor arguments, so they are `@UiField(provided = true)` or lose them in their wave.

**Agreed (24 October 2026), applying to every wave:**

1. **`setVisible` stays.** It is state, not styling (GWT also sets `aria-hidden` with it). Every other inline style goes; a block with hidden states of its own (split-pane panes, tab-bar tabs) uses BEM modifiers.
2. **Attributes through typed setters.** Templates set ARIA and the like through setters on Stroom's widgets (`ariaLabel="Filter"`, `placeholder="…"`, `opensDialog="true"`), added to each widget in its wave, as `TickBox.setAriaLabel` and `LinkButton` have.
3. **Three exceptions to "everything static in the template":** a single-element widget (a `ButtonBase`, or a `FlowPanel` or `Widget` subclass such as `ToolbarButton`) sets its root block class and role in its constructor; a GWT `PopupPanel`'s root keeps its class and role in Java; and cells keep `SafeHtmlTemplates`. Any other markup inside them goes in a template or a `SafeHtmlTemplate`.
4. **One helper for custom properties, before wave 1:** `stroom.widget.util.client.CssProperties.set(element, "--block__name", value)` and `remove(...)`, replacing the private copies in `DataGridDom`, `ClassificationLabel`, `FieldEnd` and `SplitPane`; names follow the existing `--block__property` form.

## Done means, for every unit

Agreed 24 October 2026. A unit is converted in its wave only when all of these hold; nothing is left for a later tidy.

1. **Renamed and moved.** A presenter (`FooPresenter`, `FooView`, `FooViewImpl`, `FooUiHandlers`) becomes `Foo` (the component) and `FooController`, side by side in the area's `client.component` package (e.g. `stroom.analytics.client.component`), with `FooViewImpl.ui.xml` moved and renamed to `Foo.ui.xml` beside `Foo.java`. Its view interface, `UiHandlers` and GIN binding are deleted. A widget keeps its package, and its template moves beside its Java. A class is named for what it is, not how it was made (no `Custom`, `My`, `Svg` or `Widget` in a name that says only that it was copied from GWT, drawn with SVG or is a widget: `CustomCheckBox` became `TickBox`), and its block has the same name in kebab case (`TickBox`, `tick-box`; agreed 24 October 2026, after wave 2). The *Becomes* column suggests the names; a better name found on the way is fine (an `Abstract...` base is usually merged rather than renamed). The callers move in the same commit.
2. **The template has everything that doesn't change** (except a single-element widget's root, a popup's root, and cells, which keep `SafeHtmlTemplates`; ARIA and the like through typed setters on Stroom's widgets). Structure (no fixed layout built in Java with `new FlowPanel()` and the like), CSS class names, ARIA roles and labels, and visible text, titles and placeholders, in plain English. Java keeps only what changes at runtime: values, state classes (`selected`, `disabled`), and text built from data. The *Java markup* column counts the places that set ARIA, classes or text, or build layout, in Java today.
3. **No inline styles.** No `getStyle().setX(...)`, no `style="..."` in a template, and no `setAttribute("style", ...)` (`setVisible` stays: it is state). A fixed style becomes a Stroom CSS class (an existing one first); a value only known at runtime (a column width, a splitter position, a canvas size) is set as a CSS custom property (`--block__property`) with `CssProperties.set`, and a class uses it, so an element's style holds custom properties only. The *Styles* column counts them.
4. **Earlier waves are declared, not built.** Every component from an earlier wave that the unit uses (the *Declares* column) is declared in its template (`<i:SelectionBox ui:field="type"/>`), not made in Java or handed in as a view; a smart part's controller attaches to it (`make(provider).attach(view.getFeedBox())`).
5. **Old APIs stay until their callers move.** A widget used widely keeps its GWT events (`ValueChangeEvent`, `ClickHandler`) beside its new callbacks until the last caller has moved, then they go; a presenter used widely can stay as a thin wrapper over its new controller (as `DocSelectionBoxPresenter` does).
6. **Its own stylesheet, BEM.** Each component (or widget) has its own stylesheet under `stroom-app/src/main/resources/ui/css/`, named for its block in lower-case kebab case (`selection-box.css` for the `selection-box` block, imported from `app.css`), holding only that block's rules. Its classes are BEM: `block`, `block__element`, `block--modifier`, all lower-case kebab case (`selection-box__value`, `selection-box--read-only`), and its custom properties `--block__property`. What is shared stays in the shared stylesheets: the common classes many blocks use (`max`, `form-control-border`, `allow-focus`, `sr-only`, the `dock-*` and `w-*` layout helpers), the theme's variables (`--control__border-color` and the palettes) and the base element rules. A block's rules move out of `components.css`, `stroom.css` and other shared files into its own file when it converts, and its old class names (`expanderCell`, `mainViewImpl-*`, `Button--base`) are renamed to BEM with every use (Java, templates, `SafeHtmlTemplates`, other stylesheets and the workbench's stories) in the same commit. (Agreed 24 October 2026, after wave 1.)
7. **Checked.** Its stories pass with the leak check, and the screenshots of every story that shows it match before and after (`-PworkbenchTestScreenshots`), since styles moving into classes must not change how it looks.

## How it was measured

- **Metrics, by script.** Every client class in `stroom-core-client-widget`, `stroom-core-client`, `stroom-dashboard-gwt` and `stroom-app-gwt` (2,000 classes, 310,463 lines; Ace's own code excluded): its lines, the signals that make it smart (REST, the event bus, GIN injection, owned children, static state), the client classes it uses and how many use it (fan-in), and whether another worktree is changing it (`data_grid2`: 254 client files; `copy_and_paste`: 55).
- **Review, by 14 agents**, one per partition (four for the widget module). Each grouped its classes into units (a presenter with its view interface, `ViewImpl`, `UiHandlers` and template is one unit) and classified each: kind, state, why it is smart, what stops it being a consistent dumb component, what it embeds, complexity (1 to 5) and effort (S to XL, against the pilot: `FieldEditor` S, `RuleSetSettings` M, the document picker M). Every one of the 2,000 classes is in exactly one unit.
- **Order.** A unit's level is how deep it sits: 0 if it embeds nothing to convert, else one more than the deepest unit it embeds. A planner grouped the units into 15 waves; a critic read the source of the early waves and found units placed before something they embed, misclassifications and in-flight files not flagged. Those corrections were applied (below), and the order was then checked by script: no unit that is split comes before a unit it embeds. Opening a dialog doesn't count (a controller can open an unconverted presenter dialog with `makeDialog`), nor does a dumb widget's outward tidy (adding callbacks and `setReadOnly` doesn't need what it contains converted).

## Before the waves

- Write the leaf-widget contract into COMPONENT_MODEL_DESIGN.md before wave 1. It covers: a public no-arg constructor so the widget works in templates; outputs as setOnX(Runnable/Consumer), or a Listeners<T> list where several may listen; setReadOnly(boolean) as the single read-only setter, with setEnabled kept only where it means something different; focus() not setFocus(boolean); and the widget's UiBinder Binder made with GWT.create, not injected. Also decide whether HasValue/ValueChangeEvent stays alongside the callbacks for one release, so callers can move gradually.
- Add the setOnX callback beside the old GWT event (ClickHandler, ValueChangeEvent, SelectionEvent) and then move callers. Never remove an event and move all its callers in one change, because TickBox has 67 callers and SelectionBox 108.
- Replace task counting inside widgets (implementations of TaskMonitorFactory in RefreshButton, PagerView, PagerViewWithHeading, Button and the spinners) with setRefreshing(boolean) and a small adapter. The adapter turns Contexts.TASK_MONITOR into setRefreshing calls on the widget.
- Add DOM-bubbled requests on the MenuRequest pattern for HyperlinkEvent, AskStroomAiEvent and the copy alerts, each with a relay to the bus. Cells and DataGridWidget then no longer need the event bus in their constructors.
- Add an owner-based constructor to RestDataProvider (51 callers) and to the *ResourceClient wrappers (AnnotationResourceClient, CredentialClient, NodeClient), so late replies are dropped once the owner is disposed. Do this once, before the list wave.
- Build one shared FilteredList component (QuickFilter, toolbar, DataGrid and pager) to replace QuickFilterPageView and PagerView in the roughly 14 lists that filter, plus a ToolbarGrid shell for the toolbar-above-grid floor map views.
- Make UserRefSelectionController and DocumentAssetSelectionController follow the DocSelectionController pilot, each attached to a DropDownBox in the template, so the presenter-in-a-view pattern goes away in wave 6.
- Re-key ShowPopupEvent and the HidePopup*Event classes, and the PopupManager/PopupSupport mapping, on a Widget or HasControllerNode instead of MyPresenterWidget. This is the critical path to deleting ControllerDialog, PresenterAdapter, MyPresenterWidget, View and ViewImpl.
- Merge Dialog and ResizableDialog, and the seven OkCancelContent-style button-bar classes, into one dialog chrome with no-arg construction and an onHideRequest callback, before wave 9 starts showing component dialogs.
- Build the component equivalents of the old frameworks before the units that depend on them: a dashboard pane base, after renaming stroom.dashboard.client.main.Component to DashboardPane; a settings-tab base to replace BasicSettingsTabPresenter and SettingsPresenter; a content-tab screen base to replace ContentTabPresenter and OpenContentTabEvent's presenter payload; and DocumentEditor / DocumentEditorHost recipes for DocTabPresenter shells. Make DocumentUserPermissionsTabProvider (29 callers) and MarkdownTabProvider (29 callers) work as TabbedComponent tabs.
- Find and replace the StaticEventBus callers (DateTimeBox, TimeRangeSelector and the HelpManager path) as each owner converts.
- Keep stroom-gwt/ISSUES.md current with the order, each wave's status and the in-flight dependencies, and update COMPONENT_MODEL_DESIGN.md's migration progress at the end of each wave.
- A document-tab base, so that a tab converted in waves 7 to 13 can be hosted both by `DocTabPresenter` (until wave 14) and by `DocumentEditor` (the critic: many tabs in those waves extend `DocPresenter`).
- The parts of the critic's review applied: `DynamicSettingsPane` after `DateTimeBox`; `StreamingProcessingPresenter` with `ProcessorPresenter`; the include/exclude filters after `DocRefListPresenter`; `MetaPresenter` with the data tabs it embeds (it is `FeedPresenter`'s and `PipelinePresenter`'s Data tab); `TabLayout`, `FlexLayout` and `Splitter` with the dashboard (they take `TabManager`); `PagerView`, `PagerViewWithHeading`, `WrapperView` and the dialog chrome after `data_grid2` (which moves or edits them); the floor map timeline's view and presenter together; `QueryDocPresenter` with its owner; `TableRowCell` with `AnnotationManager`; `DataGridWidget` and `TraceOverviewWidget` reclassified as smart widgets (they take the app's bus), `LinkTabPanelPresenter` as a presenter, and `DocumentAssetQuickUploadPresenter` as a dialog. `DocSelectionBoxPresenter`, a wrapper over `DocSelectionController` since the pilot, is retired in wave 8 once its users attach the controller themselves. `SelectionBox` keeps wave 2 for its outward API only; its popup and base class change in wave 5.

## 1. Conventions and simple leaves

> **Done, 24 October 2026** (commits `3ef773039c` to `b8d4fb9efa`), checked by compiling, checkstyle, the client modules' unit tests and both apps' GWT compiles; **the workbench stories and screenshots have not been run for it yet.** First `CssProperties` and the widget rules ("Widget rules" in the design). Then: `Button`, `FabButton` and `ToolbarSeparator` have templates (a `Parts` holder binds the button's element, as `ButtonBase` takes it in its constructor), and `Button` and `LinkButton` have `setOnClick`; the button's ripple, the expander's indent, the percent bar's width, the colour swatch's colour and the layered canvas's size are custom properties (`layered-canvas.css` is new); `SpinnerLarge`'s template moved beside its Java and `SpinnerSmall` has one; the main toolbar's `Spinner` is `TaskSpinner`, declaring its `SpinnerLarge`; the value spinner cell's helper `Spinner` is `SpinStepper`; `IconBox` is a component with a template, and `setReadonly` is the inherited `setReadOnly`; `SettingBlock`, `SettingsGroup` and `FormGroup` declare their parts in templates; `AssignYourselfLink` is a widget with a template; Plan B's `AbstractSettingsWidget` is a component with `setOnChange` (its `setUiHandlers` bridges to it until wave 7 moves the views).
>
> Left, on purpose: `FormGroup` still shows only the parts in use by adding and removing them (the plan's fixed parts shown and hidden change the DOM of 54 screens, so they wait for a story and screenshot run); `QuickSettings`' groups stay built in Java (a group's root is a panel whose `role` a template can't set, and its title and presets are data); `ToolTipCell` (unused) and `QueryCell`'s class literals are left for a tidy; the cells keep their `SafeHtmlTemplates`; `RefreshButton` and `SpinnerSmall` already had `setRefreshing`, `Button` `setLoading`, `SpinnerLarge` shows its state with `setVisible` and `TaskSpinner` with `start`/`stop`, so their task counting stays until their owners use those; `FieldEnd` sets its custom property inside a JavaScript `ResizeObserver` callback, so it keeps doing so there rather than calling `CssProperties`; and two optional extras wait for their callers' waves: an `IconBox` `dropDown` attribute (for templates, wave 2's `BaseSelectionBox`) and `DescriptionHTML` setting its own class.
>
> Audited the same day against `component-migration/wave-01.md`, and fixed: `LayeredCanvas` now sets its size on every layer as well as the root (the pipeline tree draws its faded layer outside the root, so it didn't inherit it), and the two tree panels add their class to the canvas instead of replacing its `layered-canvas` class; `FabButton` has `setOnClick` and `focus()`, and `TaskSpinner` `setOnClick`; the help button's "Click for help" is in `FormGroup.ui.xml`; Plan B's widgets apply the inherited `setReadOnly` through their `onReadOnly` (it did nothing before), and ignore a null change handler. The stories to check when the workbench runs: the pipeline structure with a detached element and the expression tree (canvas size and scroll), a button's ripple by mouse and keyboard, the selection, date, time and schedule boxes' icon click when read only and disabled, Assign Yourself (neither a click nor Enter opens the user chooser; disabled says why), the main toolbar's spinner, Plan B and floor map settings marking the document changed (including the metric and histogram keys' time zone), the form groups, and the percent bar and colour swatch cells, against their screenshots.

Each unit's plan (names, template, what goes, every inline style and its replacement, what moves into the template): [`component-migration/wave-01.md`](component-migration/wave-01.md).

Set the leaf contract (no-arg constructor, setOnX, setReadOnly, focus(), Binder made with GWT.create) on the smallest leaf widgets and cells that are not in flight, so every later wave has a model to copy. Buttons gain setOnClick, IconBox's setReadonly is renamed setReadOnly, and RefreshButton/Spinner* get setRefreshing in place of their own task counting.

**Why now.** Each of these units embeds nothing that needs converting, and most are S effort. Button, ToolbarButton and Toolbar together have about 180 callers, so fixing their API first unblocks almost every template above them. The read-only and callback conventions have to be settled before any composite reads Contexts.READ_ONLY.

**Risks.**

- Button and ToolbarButton have high fan-in. Keep ClickHandler/ButtonView working while setOnClick is added, and move callers in later waves.
- AbstractSettingsWidget is the base for 17 Plan B leaves. Change it to the thin Component base without breaking them; they convert in wave 7.
- RefreshButton registers a global REFRESH key binding statically. Keep that behaviour, or move it to an explicit app-level registration.

**Template work in this wave.** 8 inline styles to remove; 37 places where Java sets classes, ARIA, text or builds fixed layout, to move into templates where they don't change at runtime; 4 earlier-wave components to declare in templates.

## 2. High fan-in input and layout widgets

> **Done, 24 October 2026**, checked by compiling, checkstyle, the client modules' unit tests, both apps' GWT compiles, every story with the leak check, and a screenshot diff against wave 1's baseline. Two shared widgets let templates write what UiBinder can't on a widget's element: `TextInput` (`stroom.widget.form.client`: `type`, `placeholder`, `role`, `ariaLabel`, `ariaHasPopup`, `ariaExpanded`) and `AriaPanel` (`stroom.widget.component.client`: `role`, `tabIndex`, `ariaLabel`, `ariaHasPopup`, `ariaHidden`, `ariaLive`). Every unit has a template beside its Java but `Editor`, `SplitPane` and `TraceHistogramChart` (none planned): `TickBox` too (a `Parts` holder, as `Button` has, though the plan had none); `DaysOfWeek`'s, `Pager`'s and `TimeZoneBox`'s moved; `Calendar`'s holds the year and month rows (`DefaultMonthSelector` merged into it) and declares `CalendarGrid` (a no-arg constructor, given its calendar by `setDatePicker`); `XSDDisplayBox`'s holds its title (`XSDDisplayBoxTitle` merged into it, the unused width and height gone); `BaseSelectionBox`'s declares its `IconBox` with the field in a new `dropDown` slot (`@UiChild`). The value widgets have `setOnChange` beside their old events; `ValueSpinner` and `BaseSelectionBox` have `ariaLabel`; `TimeZoneBox` has a plain constructor and `setOnChange` (`ChangeUiHandlers`, `onReadOnly` and its injected `Binder` gone); `Histogram.setOnClick`, `FloorMapIconPicker.setOnChange` and `TraceHistogramChart.setOnZoom` (with a no-arg constructor and `setDateTimeFormatter`) are the renamed callbacks; `PasswordBox.setFocus` is `focus()`. `SelectionBoxView` and the selection box's and duration picker's `EventBinder` use are gone (handlers on their own children are added once), as is the tick box's unused word wrap. Inline styles became classes or custom properties (`SplitPane`'s tracks and hidden panes, `ScrollbarMetrics`, the XSD diagram's grid, padding, labels and occurrences, the histogram's tooltip, the trace histogram's bars, the selection box's opacity, also in `SelectionBoxCell`). Stylesheets: `radio-group.css` (from `radio-button.css`), `line-col-input.css`, `scrollbar-metrics.css`, `histogram.css` (was `stroom-histogram`), `trace-histogram-chart.css` (from `pathways.css`), `days-of-week.css` (was `days`) and `paging-bar.css` (the pager's `pager-paging`, `pager-label`, `pager-textBox` and `pager-space`; not `pager`, which is the class of the container a `PagerView` puts it in); `selection-box--readonly` is `selection-box--read-only`.
>
> Left, on purpose: `xsdbrowser.css` isn't BEM yet, as it is the XSD browser's block (wave 8, which has the list); `ColourBox`, `RadioGroup` and `FloorMapIconPicker` keep only `setEnabled` (a native colour or radio input can't be read only, and their one caller turns them off when its mode isn't in use, a state of its own), and `DaysOfWeek`, `TimeZoneBox` and `DropDownBox` keep `setEnabled` beside `setReadOnly` while anything (if only the view state stories) uses it; `PasswordBox.getInput()` and `LineColInput.getTextBox()` stay while `FieldValidity` callers reach in; `EventBinder` stays for its other users; a `ValueSpinner`'s bounds stay in Java (UiBinder can't parse a `long`); `Histogram`, `TraceHistogramChart`, `TextInput` and `AriaPanel` have no stories of their own (the last two are in every spinner, filter, picker and selection box story), and the `Widgets/Inputs/TextInput` stories are about Stroom's `TextBox`, not the new class.
>
> Audited the same day against `component-migration/wave-02.md`, and fixed: the XSD diagram's one-line labels (`new Label(text, false)`, which writes an inline `white-space`) are `xsdBrowser-oneLine`; selecting a node no longer highlights its whole grid (older, `ISSUES.md`); `PasswordBox`'s callers use `focus()`; `Pager` declares its `RefreshButton`; `TimeZoneBox` hears its parts through their `setOnChange`; and stale names in the docs, the suite's inventory and later waves' plans (`wave-05.md`, `wave-15.md`).

Each unit's plan (names, template, what goes, every inline style and its replacement, what moves into the template): [`component-migration/wave-02.md`](component-migration/wave-02.md).

Give the shared value widgets and layout widgets setOnChange callbacks and one read-only setter, alongside their existing HasValue/ValueChangeEvent API, and make them usable in templates (TimeZoneBox loses its injected Binder; DropDownBox keeps one disabled state).

**Why now.** Between them these widgets have more than 350 callers (SelectionBox 108, TickBox 67, ValueSpinner 34, QuickFilter 21). Every form, settings pane and dialog later in the plan is built from them.

**Risks.**

- SelectionBox's popup (SelectionPopup/SelectionList) is in flight in data_grid2. Change only SelectionBox's outward API here; its two-step init(model) is fixed in wave 5.
- Editor needs an explicit destroy() for its Ace instance. The component's dispose must call it, or Ace instances leak.
- Settings panes still call onReadOnly while this changes. Agree how setReadOnly and setEnabled differ before changing TickBox.

**Template work in this wave.** 24 inline styles to remove; 48 places where Java sets classes, ARIA, text or builds fixed layout, to move into templates where they don't change at runtime; 7 earlier-wave components to declare in templates.

## 3. Containers, tab bars, dialog chrome and dumb view/presenter collapses

Each unit's plan (names, template, what goes, every inline style and its replacement, what moves into the template): [`component-migration/wave-03.md`](component-migration/wave-03.md).

Move the tab-bar family to setOnSelect/setOnClose/setOnMenu, give the dialog chrome and its button bars templates and no-arg constructors, and convert the presenter/view pairs that hold no logic into components.

> **Decided before starting, 24 October 2026** (the full list is at the top of `component-migration/wave-03.md`): nothing is merged. `Dialog` and `ResizableDialog` stay two units, and the button bars four components (`OkCancelContent`, `CloseContent`, `AcceptRejectContent`, `CreateOkCancelContent`); the four `Resizable*Content` copies go. "Collapsing" a presenter means converting one with no logic into a dumb component (or a pair, if it has some). The wave starts with an `Icon` widget, so fixed icons go in templates. `WrapperView` is left for deletion when its users convert (waves 10 and 11). `DraggableTabBar` stays a class of its own, and the tab overflow button is one `TabSelector` widget (its two copies, `CurveTabSelector` and `BasicTabSelector`, and their abstract base go). It is done in three steps, each checked with every story and a screenshot diff: the dialog chrome; the tab bars; the dumb presenters and tidies.
>
> **Done, 24 October 2026**, in three steps. (1) `Icon` (`stroom.svg.client`: a picture declared in a template, the markup `SvgImageUtil` writes, `aria-hidden`); the dialog chrome: `AbstractPopupPanel`, `SimplePopup`, `Dialog` and `ResizableDialog` have no-arg constructors (the resizable one takes its size) and `setOnAction`, the dialogs' templates are beside their Java with the spinner hidden, the hint's `role="status"` and the resize handle's `Icon` in them, a dialog's size is `--dialog__width`/`--dialog__height` on its container (from `PopupUtil`, the resize handles and the window options alike) and its handles show by `dialog--resize-width`/`--resize-height`, a floating popup is shown by `popup--placed` rather than an inline opacity; the four button bars (`OkCancelContent`, `CloseContent`, `AcceptRejectContent`, `CreateOkCancelContent`, the last renamed from `ResizableCreateOkCancelContent`) have templates with their icons, no-arg constructors and `setOnHideRequest`, and serve both dialogs (`PopupSupportImpl` has one branch, not two), the three `Resizable*Content` copies gone; `HelpPopup` has a template and setters in place of its factories. (2) The tab bars: `AbstractTabBar` is a `ComplexPanel` given its root and tab list by each bar's template (`CurveTabBar.ui.xml`, `LinkTabBar.ui.xml`), places tabs and separators with `--tab-bar__left` and hides those that don't fit with `tab-bar__tab--hidden`, and has `setOnSelect` and `setOnClose` beside its events; `CurveTab` and `LinkTab` are components with templates (`role="tab"` and the rest in them, the tab's spinner declared, its cross an `Icon`, a tab that can't be closed `tab-bar__tab--fixed`); the overflow button is one `TabSelector` (`tab-selector`, `tab-selector.css`; `AbstractTabSelector`, `CurveTabSelector` and `BasicTabSelector` gone); `DraggableTabBar` stays, its dead separator code gone; `LayerContainerImpl` has a template and the block `layer-container` (`layer-container.css`). (3) `HtmlPane` (was `HtmlPresenter`) and `SourceProgressBar` (was `ProgressPresenter`; not `ProgressBar`, as `progress-bar` is a Bootstrap class that gave it a blue fill; its window's position and size custom properties, its colours modifiers, `Progress` moved to `stroom.widget.progress.client`) and `UserTask` (was `UserTaskPresenter`; Terminate a toolbar button, so no GWT button) are dumb components; `NameDialog` and `NameDialogController` (was `TextBoxPopup`) are a pair, shown with `ControllerDialog.showOnce`; `InfoTooltip` and `IFrame` are components, under `TooltipPresenter` and `IFramePresenter`, which stay as thin presenters (see below); `MainToolbar` (block `main-toolbar`), `QueryButtons` (block `query-buttons`, with the two views that borrow its look), `VisToolbarRow` (a component, block `vis-toolbar-row`) and `OperatorEditor` (a template; its width a `termEditor-item operator` class) have callbacks in place of their `UiHandlers` (`MainUiHandlers`, `QueryUiHandlers` gone). `GroupLevelButtons` needed nothing.
>
> Left, on purpose: `WrapperView` (deleted when its users convert, waves 10 and 11); `InfoTooltipController` and `IFrameController`: an owner keeps one tooltip and shows it again (a second click on its icon closes it), and the link's page as a dialog renames itself as the page's title changes (`RenamePopupEvent`), which `ControllerDialog.showOnce` (one use, the presenter that shows it hidden) can't do; so `TooltipPresenter` and `IFramePresenter` stay as thin presenters over the components (as `DocSelectionBoxPresenter` does) until the popup manager can show a controller again or its owners convert (lists, wave 10; the hyperlink handler, wave 15); `CurveTabLayoutViewImpl`'s inline `right` (wave 15); the tab bar's menu events have no callbacks yet (they are only forwarded to the bus by their owners, until wave 15); the suite's generated `route-recipes.json` still names `TextBoxPopup` until its tool is run again.
>
> Audited 25 October 2026 against `component-migration/wave-03.md`, and fixed: the splash screen's Accept had lost its green when the button bars merged (it is `button--contained-green`, with a story); menus and help popups closed on Back and Forward again (`setAutoHideEnabled` doesn't set that, as GWT's constructor did); a data or processor screen's links opened once for every load or selection (`OpenLinkUtil.addClickHandler` was added each time; it returns its registration, owned once, with a story; older); the tab bar's `TabSelector` is adopted, so attached (it was only appended), and the bars' `clear()` removes its tabs as widgets; `TabBar` has `setOnSelect` and `setOnClose`; the dialog's title icon is an `Icon` in its template; `HelpPopup` has `setOnClose`; `QueryButtons.setSearching` is called (and it is no longer a `SearchStateListener`); the vis toolbar's press callback is set once (`setOnVisButtonPress`); `NameDialog` uses `TextInput`, and its OK and Cancel handling is tested on the JVM again (`TestNameDialogController`) and Cancel in a story; `SourceProgressBar` has an Unbounded story and plays checking its states and click; inline styles in `DataPresenter` (`max`) and `QueryPresenter` (`toolbar-button--invisible`); the user task screen's REST calls have an owner; javadoc; the suite's `gwt-inventory` regenerated. Decided, not done (in `component-migration/wave-03.md`): `Icon` writes the shared `svg-image` block, not one of its own; the `TabSelector` is made in Java, as an element-rooted template can't hold widgets; `draggable` stays with `DraggableTabBar`, which handles the drag; the rest is under "Audited" there.


**Why now.** These are leaf-level containers and chrome. The tab bars block CurveTabLayoutPresenter and the 16 views built on LinkTabsLayoutView. The dialog chrome blocks every component dialog in waves 9 and 11. PagerView (86 callers) and WrapperView (11 callers) must exist as components before the lists and tool screens can move (PagerView moved to wave 5; WrapperView is deleted instead, see above).

**Risks.**

- AbstractTabBar is 1,349 lines with four GwtEvent outputs. Its owners forward these to the bus, so keep both routes until CurveTabLayoutPresenter converts (wave 15).
- PagerView and WrapperView are widely used. Ship the component next to the old view and keep the GIN binding until wave 10 or 15 moves the callers. (Both left this wave: PagerView is wave 5's, and WrapperView goes with its users.)
- TooltipPresenter is used as a singleton by NodeStatusList and ProcessorTask*. It must keep working as an app-wide helper made through AppRoot.

**Template work in this wave.** 16 inline styles to remove; 33 places where Java sets classes, ARIA, text or builds fixed layout, to move into templates where they don't change at runtime; 9 earlier-wave components to declare in templates.

## 4. Smart widgets not in flight: split into box plus controller

Each unit's plan (names, template, what goes, every inline style and its replacement, what moves into the template): [`component-migration/wave-04.md`](component-migration/wave-04.md).

Split the widgets that open their own presenter dialogs, or reach services or the bus themselves, into a dumb box and a controller or callback, following the DropDownBox and DocSelectionController pilot. The date, time and schedule popups become component dialogs. The navigators invert to callbacks. ClassificationLabel takes its colours from outside, and VisFrame and CustomFileUpload lose their bus and transport coupling.

> **Done, 25 October 2026**: all 17 units (`VisFrame`, which the table lists in wave 5, stays there). The boxes (`TimeBox`, `DateTimeBox`, `ScheduleBox`, `TimeRangeSelector`) only ask for their dialogs, and a controller attached by each screen opens a one-use picker controller (`TimePicker`, `DateTimePicker`, `SchedulePicker`, `TimeRangePicker`); every date-time box user attaches one, so the `StaticEventBus` fallbacks are gone. `CustomFileUpload` is `FileChooser` with a `FileChooserController`; the navigators share `NavigatorAction`; `ClassificationLabel` has one `classification-label` block. What was decided on the way, and what is left, is at the top of [`component-migration/wave-04.md`](component-migration/wave-04.md).
>
> Audited 25 October 2026, and fixed: requests made through clients that take a task monitor (the schedule dialog's times) showed no progress where nothing provided one, as controllers passed the context's do-nothing default (`Controller.getProvidedTaskMonitor()` is null then, so Stroom's own busy indicator shows; two earlier controllers did the same); a visualisation's settings kept every date controller of every visualisation chosen (released with their tabs); the item and source range dialogs read their numbers as `int`s; `TimeRangeSelector` has the `setOnChange` the notes claimed, and the read-only and Set Location OK checks they claimed are written; and the rest (copyright years, javadoc, stale names, weak story checks) is in `wave-04.md`'s Audited section.

**Why now.** The input widgets from wave 2 are now dumb. DateTimeBox (19 callers) and ScheduleBox are what stop the floor map, credential and scheduling views from being used in templates: those views inject a Provider<DateTimePopup> only to pass it on. Removing the StaticEventBus fallbacks here closes a hidden smart dependency.

**Risks.**

- DateTimeBox's popup provider is injected in several views (CredentialSettings, ReplaceSecret, FloorMapObjectEdit, FloorMapTimelineSettings). Keep setPopupProvider until each of them converts.
- VisFrame holds static state (the PostMessage singleton, frame id counters). Keep it a widget with a dispose step, and test with both of its users (VisPresenter and QueryResultVisPresenter).
- CustomFileUpload's upload transport is swapped by workbench stories. Keep the transport hook test-only when moving the upload into a controller.

**Template work in this wave.** 4 inline styles to remove; 44 places where Java sets classes, ARIA, text or builds fixed layout, to move into templates where they don't change at runtime; 7 earlier-wave components to declare in templates.

## 5. Data grid, cells and selection list

Each unit's plan (names, template, what goes, every inline style and its replacement, what moves into the template): [`component-migration/wave-05.md`](component-migration/wave-05.md).

> **Done, 25 October 2026**: all 16 units, in 9 commits. The cells ask through callbacks (`RefCellsController` sets the ref cells'), `SvgCell` is `IconCell`, and `DataGrid` has a template, no inline styles and no bus: its context menu, links and Stroom AI questions go up the page as requests (`MenuRequest`, `OpenHyperlinkRequest`, `AskStroomAiTableRequest`) to the relays of `CorePresenter` (which, unlike `MainPresenter`, the standalone dashboard shows). `PagerView` is `PagerPanel` (and `HeadedPagerPanel`), `SelectionList` and `SelectionPopup` take `setModel` and `setOnClose`, and `VisFrame` has a `VisFrameController`. Two bugs fixed on the way, both on master too: a dashboard opened from a table's menu got no parent dashboard, and the standalone dashboard's menus never opened. What was decided, and what is left (the grid's sort, range and selection outputs), is at the top of [`component-migration/wave-05.md`](component-migration/wave-05.md).

> Audited 25 October 2026, and fixed: the Add Column list added a column twice after its button closed it (on master too, the next choice; a changelog entry); every selection list showed the grid's own context menu on a right-click (its grid's private bus had hidden it); showing a header hidden after the first render didn't draw it again; `MessagePanel` and `VisFrame` have their own BEM stylesheets; the counts and several claims in the notes; and the rest (stale names the renames left, the design history they rewrote, a "Since wave 5" note in each later plan) is in `wave-05.md`'s Audited section, with what was decided and left.

Make DataGridWidget usable in templates and free of the bus: requests bubble through the DOM, and sort, range and selection outputs become callbacks. Cells take onOpen/onMenu callbacks instead of an EventBus or HasHandlers. SelectionList and SelectionPopup move from init(model) to setters.

**Why now.** DataGridWidget has 114 callers and sits under every list. Nearly all of these files are being changed in data_grid2 or copy_and_paste, so this wave starts as soon as those land, before any list.

**Risks.**

- Blocked until data_grid2 and copy_and_paste are merged. Starting earlier means conflicts in nearly every file in the wave.
- The ref cells and TableRowCell open menus and documents. The menu-opener and onOpen callbacks need a working route to the bus through the owning list, or context menus stop working.
- Changing DataGridWidget's constructor touches 114 callers. Keep a deprecated constructor that takes the bus until wave 10.

**Template work in this wave.** 22 inline styles to remove; 15 places where Java sets classes, ARIA, text or builds fixed layout, to move into templates where they don't change at runtime; 7 earlier-wave components to declare in templates.

## 6. Editor, menu, tree engine, explorer tree and the remaining pickers

Each unit's plan (names, template, what goes, every inline style and its replacement, what moves into the template): [`component-migration/wave-06.md`](component-migration/wave-06.md).

> **Done, 25 October 2026**: all 16 units, in 11 commits. The tree engine is configured with callbacks, and the explorer tree is one `ExplorerTree` fed by an `ExplorerTreeController`. The pickers attach a selection controller to a `DropDownBox` (`DocumentAssetSelectionController`, `UserRefSelectionController`), and `DropDownView` is gone. The annotation choosers are one `Chooser` shown by one-use `ChooserController` and `MultiChooserController` popups. The menu is `MenuController`, shown with `ControllerDialog`. `TraceOverviewWidget` has a template and no inline styles. The editor is a `CodeEditor` with `CodeEditorOptions`, `CodeEditorController` and `AceEditorController`, and `EditorPresenter` is a thin wrapper for its 31 callers. One bug fixed on the way, on master too: a trace's Span Info couldn't be scrolled with the keyboard. What was decided, and what is left, is at the top of [`component-migration/wave-06.md`](component-migration/wave-06.md).

Turn the Ace EditorPresenter into a dumb CodeEditor with a settings model in place of its 13 option getters. Split MenuPresenter into a component. Configure DraggableTreePanel and PipelineTreePanel with callbacks instead of subclassing. Split AbstractExplorerTree into a tree widget and an ExplorerTreeModel controller. Give FlexLayout and TabLayout no-arg construction. Finish the UserRef and DocumentAsset box splits so DropDownViewImpl can go.

**Why now.** Everything at level 1 and above that embeds an editor (31 callers), the explorer tree, a user picker (11 callers) or the tree engine waits on these units. Wave 5 has made the grid and selection list dumb, which the choosers and the explorer popup need.

**Risks.**

- The EditorPresenter API change is the hardest in the plan: callers change editor options directly through 13 getXxxOption() getters. Introduce the settings model and move callers in the same review.
- DraggableTreePanel is shared by the pipeline tree and the query expression tree. Test both, including the agreed keyboard designs.
- Making MenuPresenter a component depends on popup events keyed on widgets (shared work). It is also in flight in copy_and_paste.
- FlexLayout is 2,498 lines and in flight. Limit the change to construction and outputs; do not restructure it in this wave.

**Template work in this wave.** 32 inline styles to remove; 26 places where Java sets classes, ARIA, text or builds fixed layout, to move into templates where they don't change at runtime; 12 earlier-wave components to declare in templates.

## 7. Composite families converted together

Each unit's plan (names, template, what goes, every inline style and its replacement, what moves into the template): [`component-migration/wave-07.md`](component-migration/wave-07.md).

> **Done, 26 October 2026**: all 52 units, in 13 commits, and wave 11's `FloorMapLayerStylePresenter` with its view. Plan B's settings are components (`PlanBTypeSettingsEditor`s declaring the leaves); the secrets are one sealed `SecretForm` with a `KeyStoreSecretFormController`; the floor map's dialogs, dock and canvas are components with controllers, and its Layers panel one component with none (`ControllerDialog.show` shows one made once, again and again); `PipelineTree` and `PipelinePathBar` share an `AbstractPipelineTree`; `PathwayTree` has a controller; the expression tree is `ExpressionTree` with `ExpressionTreeController`, each `TermEditor` given a `TermEditorController`, and both `EditExpressionPresenter`s are one `ExpressionEditor`. One bug fixed on the way (master has it too): a press on a row in the floor map's lists selected nothing. What was decided, and what is left (BEM names for the floor map, term editor and expression tree classes), is at the top of [`component-migration/wave-07.md`](component-migration/wave-07.md). **Audited, 26 October 2026**: seven reviewers; fixed two regressions on this branch (a group's highlight eye selecting it, a term's document picker 1px low), a pathway bug master has too, BEM names (`secret-form`, `pathway-tree`, `pipeline-path-bar`), inline ARIA and placeholders into templates, the canvas's animation loop on dispose, and 17 stories; see "Audited" in the wave file.

Convert the regular stacks in one pass each: the 17 Plan B leaf settings widgets and their 8 type-settings presenters become dumb components (no controller); the floor map leaf panels are converted together with their ViewImpls; the five credential secret forms move behind a shared SecretForm; the pipeline and pathway trees; and the expression tree (TermEditor, then ExpressionTreePanel, then ExpressionTreePresenter, then both EditExpressionPresenters).

**Why now.** These are the first composites. Each family embeds only widgets that are dumb by now: the wave 2 inputs, the wave 4 date and time boxes, and the wave 6 tree engine and pickers. Each family is regular enough that converting it in one sweep is cheaper than doing it piece by piece.

**Risks.**

- This is a large wave. Review it as five sub-batches: Plan B, floor map, secrets, pipeline/pathway trees, and the expression tree.
- FloorMapCanvasViewImpl (3,104 lines) and FloorMapCanvasPresenter (3,712 lines) are XL. Rename the callbacks and remove the view interface without restructuring the drawing code.
- Plan B's carry-over of common values across a state-type change currently works through instanceof checks on views. Move that into plain data held by PlanBSettingsPresenter (wave 12) and do not break it in the meantime.
- EditExpressionPresenter has 12 callers that use init(RestFactory, ...). Replace init with setters and a REST owner, and keep the read-only and change behaviour.

**Template work in this wave.** 19 inline styles to remove; 51 places where Java sets classes, ARIA, text or builds fixed layout, to move into templates where they don't change at runtime; 15 earlier-wave components to declare in templates.

## 8. Composite forms and panes built on the new pickers and editors

Each unit's plan (names, template, what goes, every inline style and its replacement, what moves into the template): [`component-migration/wave-08.md`](component-migration/wave-08.md).

> **Done and audited, 26 October 2026**: all units but `DocSelectionBoxPresenter` (20 classes in later waves, with 24 pickers, still use the wrapper), in 25 commits. The explorer chooser is `ExplorerPopup` with a controller, used again and again or once; a `DocTabPresenter`'s tab can show a controller (`ControllerTabProvider`), as the Documentation tab does through `MarkdownTabProvider`; the settings tabs, the preferences' and Ask Stroom AI config's tabs, the code editor panes (the data viewer's and query result's text, the column filter, the email templates, Edit Comment, Words, the Markdown editor) and the query toolbar, query help, XSD browser, content pack details and floor map timeline are components with controllers. Three bugs fixed on the way (master has each): the floor map event expiry's fields had no names, an XML schema's Home, Back and Forward threw when nothing had been shown, and the content pack details' links were told apart by colour alone. The audit fixed a regression (an XSD's occurrences lost their class), three bugs master has too (an Elastic index's rerank score suffix not making it changed, a read-only pathways document's pickers opening, the Markdown preview not following a theme change) and the callbacks' names. What was decided, where it differs from the plans, is at the top of [`component-migration/wave-08.md`](component-migration/wave-08.md).

Convert the settings forms, editor panes and explorer pickers that embed DocSelectionBox, UserRef, EditorPresenter, Markdown or the explorer tree. Replace embedded DocSelectionBoxPresenter with a DropDownBox in the template plus DocSelectionController. Views stop exposing their widgets, and onReadOnly becomes setReadOnly.

**Why now.** Their children (editor, explorer tree, pickers, secret forms and Plan B leaves) became components in waves 6 and 7. MarkdownEditPresenter (34 callers) and DocSelectionPopup (11 callers) unblock many tabs and dialogs.

**Risks.**

- The explorer popup is the foundation of DocSelectionController. Any regression here breaks every document picker, so run the picker stories with the leak check.
- Settings panes are tabs on documents that have not converted yet. They must still work when hosted by DocPresenter or DocTabPresenter (presenters can host components).
- QueryHelpPresenter is handed the EditorPresenter. Replace that with an onInsert(text) callback the owner wires up.

**Template work in this wave.** 35 inline styles to remove; 60 places where Java sets classes, ARIA, text or builds fixed layout, to move into templates where they don't change at runtime; 29 earlier-wave components to declare in templates.

## 9. Near-dumb small dialogs

Each unit's plan (names, template, what goes, every inline style and its replacement, what moves into the template): [`component-migration/wave-09.md`](component-migration/wave-09.md).

> **Done and audited, 26 October 2026**: all 42 units, in 17 commits and the audit's. Each dialog is a component with a controller made for one use and shown with `ControllerDialog.showOnce` from a method that takes its result's callback (Download Options and Current Selection are kept and shown again); the name dialogs share one `NameEntry`, the six filter dialogs one `ExpressionDialog`, and `AlertPlugin` keeps the alert queue (`CommonAlertController` shows one; a prompt is the browser's). Six bugs fixed on the way (master has each): a refused new asset name's message, Edit Element's description, a one-theme custom row style, and the pathway Constraint dialog's New, rename, ranges and bad values. The audit fixed two regressions (a GIN cycle that stopped the app's GWT compile, and constraint text trimmed), two bugs master has too (a blank Git Push message accepted, Edit Property losing a changed value), put Stroom's `TextInput`/`TextAreaInput` in every component template, and made the alert's and the shortcut recorder's classes BEM; then Stroom's widgets were put wherever Stroom has its own version of one of GWT's, and checked by checkstyle and `TestStroomWidgetsOnly`. What was decided, where it differs from the plans, is at the top of [`component-migration/wave-09.md`](component-migration/wave-09.md).

Collapse each small dialog (presenter, view interface and ViewImpl) into one component dialog. Each gets a show or edit(..., callback) method and is shown with ComponentDialog.showOnce. Validation returns a message, and the opener raises the alert. Callers stop reading getView() getters.

**Why now.** The dialog chrome (wave 3) and the forms and pickers they embed (waves 7-8) are ready. Most of these are S effort quick wins in the style of the FieldEditor pilot, and they remove the shared NameDocumentView (7 callers) and the back-references into parent presenters (TablePresenter, DashboardPresenter).

**Risks.**

- CommonAlertPresenter queues alerts and runs their callbacks on hide. Keep the queue order and the callback timing exactly.
- One shared name dialog replaces NameDocumentView for five presenters. Each owner's REST create call moves into its owner or controller, not into the dialog.
- Callers that read getView() getters inside HidePopupRequestEvent handlers must change in the same commit as their dialog.

**Template work in this wave.** 19 inline styles to remove; 39 places where Java sets classes, ARIA, text or builds fixed layout, to move into templates where they don't change at runtime; 7 earlier-wave components to declare in templates.

## 10. Lists

Each unit's plan (names, template, what goes, every inline style and its replacement, what moves into the template): [`component-migration/wave-10.md`](component-migration/wave-10.md).

Convert every list presenter built on PagerView/DataGridWidget into a list component plus a controller that owns its REST data: RestDataProvider with an owner, and FilteredList where the list has a quick filter. Selection goes out through callbacks, and back-references to parents are removed. The floor map grid lists use the shared ToolbarGrid.

**Why now.** The grid and cells (wave 5) and the RestDataProvider owner change (shared work) are in place, and data_grid2 has landed, since it touches nearly every unit here. Lists sit under every screen, document tab and larger dialog. A converted list can still open its unconverted edit dialog with makeDialog, so dialog ownership does not block this wave.

**Risks.**

- This is the largest wave (89 units). Review it by area: explorer/find, pipeline/index fields, processing/meta, nodes/jobs/cache/volumes, analytics/dashboard lists, security/users, floor map.
- It cannot start until data_grid2 has merged. Check its final API before writing FilteredList.
- Lists that call back into a parent (ScheduledProcess*, ContentStoreContentPackList, MarkerList) need the parent's handling replaced with callbacks the parent wires, even though the parent converts later.

**Template work in this wave.** 23 inline styles to remove; 61 places where Java sets classes, ARIA, text or builds fixed layout, to move into templates where they don't change at runtime; 120 earlier-wave components to declare in templates.

## 11. Larger dialogs (REST-backed, owning lists or forms)

Each unit's plan (names, template, what goes, every inline style and its replacement, what moves into the template): [`component-migration/wave-11.md`](component-migration/wave-11.md).

> **Done and audited, 27 October 2026**: all 81 units (`FloorMapLayerStylePresenter` was wave 7's),
> in 58 commits and the audit's 5. Each dialog is a component with a controller, made for each use
> or kept by its owner; one that fetches before it shows disposes itself when the fetch fails. The
> permissions tab's main content tab stays a thin presenter shell until wave 15. Five reviewers
> audited the wave: four regressions on this branch fixed (the credential tabs' tab panel, the volume
> group dialog opening twice, a second Task Manager, group edits' progress), seven leaks closed and
> the leftover shared classes given BEM stylesheets. See `wave-11.md`.

Split the dialogs that call REST, own lists or forms, or make further dialogs into a component dialog plus a controller. Results go back through callbacks, the controller supplies the task monitor from context, and the identity flows hang off ChangePasswordPresenter.

**Why now.** Their embedded lists (wave 10), forms and pickers (waves 7-8) and small child dialogs (wave 9) are components now. DocumentUserPermissionsPresenter (8 callers) is the permissions tab that most document editors need in wave 14.

**Risks.**

- Review by area: identity, explorer and import/export, volumes/nodes/processing, security/permissions, annotations, query/dashboard dialogs, floor map.
- The identity screens run before the main app has started (login, reset password). Test them outside the workbench shell as well.
- The UiHandlers that carry a TaskMonitorFactory (permissions edits) must take the task monitor from context. Watch that the spinners still show.

**Template work in this wave.** 28 inline styles to remove; 109 places where Java sets classes, ARIA, text or builds fixed layout, to move into templates where they don't change at runtime; 69 earlier-wave components to declare in templates.

## 12. Tabs and composite panes (excluding query and dashboard)

Each unit's plan (names, template, what goes, every inline style and its replacement, what moves into the template): [`component-migration/wave-12.md`](component-migration/wave-12.md).

> **Done and audited, 27 October 2026**: all 32 units, in 28 commits and the audit's 3. Each tab or
> pane is a component with a controller; a document shows one through `ControllerTabProvider`, or
> `ControllerTabProvider.ofOwned` for one it owns, and the analytic Execution tab is one
> `AnalyticProcessing` for rules and reports. The processor tasks and user groups content tabs stay
> thin presenter shells until wave 15. Four reviewers audited the wave: the stepping screen's
> unneeded Ace editors and unreleased elements fixed, the annotation editor given callbacks in place
> of its owner, late replies guarded. The full workbench run after it passed all 1,716 stories with
> the leak check. See `wave-12.md`.

Convert document tabs and large composite panes into components with controllers, removing the dependency on DocPresenter: data, source and stepping; pipeline structure; annotations; processing and notifications; Plan B settings; user and permissions tabs; retention and processor tabs. Hand-rolled tabs become TabbedComponent.

**Why now.** Everything these tabs embed (lists, forms, the editor, trees and dialogs) is converted. The document editors in wave 14 need their tab bodies as components first.

**Risks.**

- Several XL units are carved into sub-components before the split: AnnotationEditPresenter (2,202 lines, in flight in copy_and_paste), DataPresenter (1,622), SteppingPresenter (1,326), PipelineStructurePresenter (1,001) and DocumentAssetPresenter (1,156).
- These tabs are still hosted by DocTabPresenter until wave 14. Dirty state and read-only must come through Contexts in both hosts.
- AbstractNotification, AbstractProcessing and AbstractDuplicateManagement are thin base and subclass pairs. Merge them into one component used by both documents rather than carrying the inheritance over.

**Template work in this wave.** 27 inline styles to remove; 50 places where Java sets classes, ARIA, text or builds fixed layout, to move into templates where they don't change at runtime; 44 earlier-wave components to declare in templates.

## 13. Query and dashboard stack

Each unit's plan (names, template, what goes, every inline style and its replacement, what moves into the template): [`component-migration/wave-13.md`](component-migration/wave-13.md).

> **Done and audited, 27 October 2026**: all 69 units, in 12 commits and the audit's 1. Each pane,
> settings tab, results table, data tab and screen of the query and dashboard stack is a component
> with a controller, on `DashboardComponentController` for panes; the dashboard's layout widgets
> have templates and its tab and annotation menus are widgetless controllers. The old component
> bases stay for `EmbeddedQueryPresenter` in wave 14. Five reviewers audited the wave: the floor map
> editor's dirty state, the data tabs' queries left polling on close and late replies fixed. Two
> bugs master has were fixed: the Key/Value Input's rename and the floor map editor's Fact and Time
> lists. The full workbench run after it passed all 1,722 stories with the leak check. See
> `wave-13.md`.

Rename the dashboard Component interface to DashboardPane, give panes and their settings a component-model base, then convert bottom-up through this stack: settings tabs, TextInput, KeyValueInput and ListInput panes, Vis, table rules and filters, ColumnsManager, TablePresenter, QueryResultTable, QueryResultTableSplit, the query-data tabs, QueryEdit, QueryDocEdit, then DashboardPresenter and EmbeddedQuery. The model pushes data through setData and setSearching, not ResultComponent.

**Why now.** This is the deepest stack (levels 2-27) and depends on almost everything below it: the expression tree, the lists, the column dialogs and the editor. It is also the most entangled with in-flight work, so it comes last before the document editors.

**Risks.**

- TablePresenter (1,762 lines), QueryResultTablePresenter (2,078), ColumnsManager (1,223), QueryPresenter and the floor map map and editor presenters (about 2,200 lines each) are XL and in flight in data_grid2 and copy_and_paste. Each needs sub-components carved out first.
- The name clash between DashboardPane and Component must be resolved before any pane extends the new Component.
- MultiRulesPresenter uses makeDialog for children it embeds in a panel. Change these to make(...) and own them while converting.
- The floor map map and editor presenters call static helpers on FloorMapQueryPresenter. Move those helpers to a plain utility class first.

**Template work in this wave.** 30 inline styles to remove; 71 places where Java sets classes, ARIA, text or builds fixed layout, to move into templates where they don't change at runtime; 50 earlier-wave components to declare in templates.

## 14. Document editors

Each unit's plan (names, template, what goes, every inline style and its replacement, what moves into the template): [`component-migration/wave-14.md`](component-migration/wave-14.md).

> **Done and audited, 28 October 2026**: all 36 units, and the statistics store's editor, in 2
> commits and the audit's 1. Every document editor is a `DocumentEditor` (on `TabbedController`)
> shown in a `DocumentEditorHost`; `DocPresenter`, `DocTabPresenter`, `LinkTabPanelPresenter` and
> the tab providers are gone, and the link tab panel is a component. Five reviewers audited the
> wave: default tabs, the dashboard's label and stale names fixed. Two bugs master has were fixed:
> any document's save saved the receipt rules, and an annotation's tab kept its old title. The full
> workbench run after it passed all 1,723 stories with the leak check. See `wave-14.md`.

Move every DocTabPresenter shell onto DocumentEditor<D> plus DocumentEditorHost, with TabbedComponent tabs (Markdown and permissions tabs as reusable tabs), then delete DocPresenter, DocTabPresenter and LinkTabPanelPresenter. RuleSetPresenter's hand-rolled tabs also move onto DocumentEditor.

**Why now.** Their tab bodies converted in waves 8, 12 and 13. The recipe is mechanical and shared, so S3ConfigPresenter, the smallest, goes first as the template. The old document framework is deleted only once no editor uses it.

**Risks.**

- The document plugins' createEditor() and OpenDocumentEvent carry presenters. They must accept DocumentEditorHost before the old bases are deleted.
- Deleting DocPresenter, DocTabPresenter and LinkTabPanelPresenter must wait for every in-flight worktree to land and for a full screenshot comparison (removals rule).
- PipelinePresenter and the dashboard and query document editors have permission-dependent tabs and multi-instance behaviour (HasMultipleInstances). Cover both with stories.

**Template work in this wave.** 0 inline styles to remove; 5 places where Java sets classes, ARIA, text or builds fixed layout, to move into templates where they don't change at runtime; 2 earlier-wave components to declare in templates.

## 15. Content-tab screens and the shell

Each unit's plan (names, template, what goes, every inline style and its replacement, what moves into the template): [`component-migration/wave-15.md`](component-migration/wave-15.md).

> **Done and audited, 28 October 2026** (overnight, decisions to check): every unit, in 1 commit
> with the audit's fixes. Each content tab is a controller on `ContentTabController`, with a
> component where it has one; the shell (Core, FullScreen, the content tab pane with
> `CurveTabLayout`, Main, Navigation) and Ask Stroom AI are components with controllers;
> `ContentTabPresenter`, `CurveTabLayoutPresenter`, `QuickFilterPageView`, `FindView` and
> `WrapperView` are gone. Five reviewers audited the wave: a tab session restore that stopped at
> the first content tab (the wave's own) and Ask Stroom AI's attached data chip shown as HTML
> (master too) were fixed. The full workbench run after it passed all 1,723 stories with the leak
> check. `MyPresenterWidget` and the presenter framework stay until agreed. See `wave-15.md`.

Convert the tool screens onto a content-tab component base: Users, Accounts, API keys, Credentials, Nodes, Jobs, Cache, Volumes, Meta, Retention, Content Store and so on. Then convert the shell: ContentTabPane, CurveTabLayout, Navigation, Main, Core, FullScreen and TabSessionManager. Replace explorer bus events with callbacks and context where possible, and finally delete ContentTabPresenter, WrapperView's GIN binding and the popup bridges.

**Why now.** Screens only own lists, dialogs and tabs, all converted by now. The shell owns the screens and the tab pane, so it goes last. NavigationPresenter can only drop its explorer bus events once nothing else listens for them.

**Risks.**

- AskStroomAiPresenter (1,543 lines, in flight) and ExecutionScheduleManager (786 lines) need sub-components carved out first.
- OpenContentTabEvent, DockEvent and the tab events carry presenters. Re-key them on components before ContentTabPresenter is deleted.
- The shell's startup order (Core, then Main, then Navigation and the tab session restore) is fragile. Test login, tab restore, maximise and full screen end to end with the workbench leak check.
- Deleting MyPresenterWidget, View and ViewImpl (452 users), PresenterAdapter and ControllerDialog is the final step. It waits for the removals rule and a full screenshot comparison.

**Template work in this wave.** 12 inline styles to remove; 45 places where Java sets classes, ARIA, text or builds fixed layout, to move into templates where they don't change at runtime; 38 earlier-wave components to declare in templates.

## 16. The last presenters and the presenter framework

Each unit's plan: [`component-migration/wave-16.md`](component-migration/wave-16.md). Added 28 October 2026, after wave 15.

> **Done and audited, 28 October 2026**: every unit, in 9 commits and 1 with the audit's fixes (a dialog shown again hid its owner's task monitor once hidden; the wave's own). Nothing is a presenter, and the presenter framework (`MyPresenterWidget`, `View`, `ViewImpl`, `UiHandlers`, `PresenterAdapter`, `AbstractPresenterModule`) is deleted; the popup, content tab and full screen events and the open callback carry a `Controller`. Two full workbench runs with the leak check (before and after the deletion) passed, and both apps GWT compile. See `wave-16.md`.

Waves 3, 6, 8 and 14 left `TooltipPresenter`, `IFramePresenter`, `EditorPresenter`, `UserRefSelectionBoxPresenter`, `DocSelectionBoxPresenter` and `DocumentEditorHost` as presenters over their new controllers, and `LinkTabsPresenter` was deferred for deletion. Re-key the popup events (`ShowPopupEvent` and the rest) and the content-tab and full screen events on `Controller`; move the wrappers' last callers and delete them; make `DocumentEditorHost` a `LayerController` and the open callback a `Consumer<Controller>`; move the workbench's helpers and coverage check off presenters; delete the unused `UiHandlers` and the deferred dead code; then delete `MyPresenterWidget`, `View`, `ViewImpl`, `UiHandlers`, `PresenterAdapter` and `AbstractPresenterModule`.

**Why now.** Nothing in the app is a presenter but these, and every screen that shows them is a controller.

**Risks.**

- The popup manager's toggle (showing a popup again hides it) and the dialog's task monitor change with the events' key; the tooltip and Ask Stroom AI stories check them.
- The tab session restore and Save on close go through the document host and the open callback.
- The framework's deletion is checked with a full workbench run, the leak check and a screenshot diff before and after.

## 17. Tidy up: packages, names and CSS

Each step's plan: [`component-migration/wave-17.md`](component-migration/wave-17.md). Added 28 October 2026, after wave 15.

> **Done and audited, 28 October 2026**: in 41 commits, each a move or a rename, and the audit's fixes (the main screen's block is `main-screen`; dead CSS, comments and doc paths). No class is in a `presenter` or `view` package, nothing is named for a presenter, and the classes named after deleted views are BEM blocks in their own stylesheets. A full workbench run passed all 1,722 stories with the leak check and the screenshots unchanged. See `wave-17.md`.

Nothing a user sees changes. Move the classes still in `client.presenter` and `client.view` packages (about 300, checked for clashes) to their area's `client.component` (components, widgets, controllers) or `client` (everything else), and merge the widget library's `presenter` and `view` packages into their parents; rename what still says "presenter", "view" or "widget" (classes, then about 140 field and variable names); make the 35 or so CSS class blocks still named after deleted views BEM blocks in their components' stylesheets, and give the eight stylesheets not in kebab case their blocks' names; then the empty directories and the docs.

**Why now.** Once nothing is a presenter, the old package and class names only mislead.

**Risks.**

- Moves touch about 1,200 files and 130 templates' `urn:import` lines: each commit is only a move, so it can be checked by the rename.
- CSS renames can change the look if a selector is missed: a screenshot diff after each must show no change.

## 18. Handlers wired in Java

Plan and record: [`component-migration/wave-18.md`](component-migration/wave-18.md). Added 28 October
2026, after wave 17.

> **Done, 28 October 2026**: the 191 `@UiHandler` methods are wired in their components'
> constructors (`setOnClick`, `setOnChange` or `addXxxHandler`), and checkstyle refuses
> `@UiHandler`. See `wave-18.md`.

Nothing a user sees changes. Each `@UiHandler` method is wired in its component's constructor,
straight after the template is bound: a Stroom button's `setOnClick`, a tick box's, spinner's or
radio group's `setOnChange` where nothing else sets it, otherwise the widget's `addXxxHandler`.

**Why now.** The waves are done, so every component can follow the rule new ones already do.

**Risks.**

- A callback holds one function, so one set by the component and again by its caller would replace
  it: the conversion uses `addXxxHandler` where the widget is handed out.
- Checked by both apps' GWT compiles and a full workbench run with the leak check.
